S.O. 594.—In exercise of the powers conferred by Sec 
tion 3 of the Delhi Special Police Establishment Act, 1946 
(Act No. 25 of 1946), the Central Government hereby spe¬ 
cifies the following offences and classes of offences for 'the 
purpose of the said section, namely :— 

(a) Offences punishable under Sections 120-B and 436 
of Ranbir Penal Code Sarnvnt 1989 (Act No. II 
1989) (1932 AD). 

(b) Attempts, abetments and conspiracies in relation to 
or in connection with one or more of the offences 
mentioned above and any other offence or offences 
committed In the course of the same transaction.

New Delhi, the 4th February, 19,92 

S.O. 597.—In pursuance of clause (a) of the sub-section 
(1) and of sub-section (2) of section 6 of the Industrial 
Development Bank of India Act, 1964 (18 of 1964), the 
Central Government hereby appoints Shri S. H. Khan, pre¬ 
sently Executive Director of Industrial Development Bank 
of India, as the Managing Director of the Industrial Deye- , 
lopment Bank of India, vide Shri S. 5. Nadkarni, for a period 
of five years with effect from the date of his taking charge. 

In partial modification to this Department's Notifications 
No. 9/48/88-BO,1(1) and (2) dated 4th October, 1988, Shri 
S. S. Nadkarni, presently the Chairman and Managing Direc¬ 
tor of Industrial Development Bank of India, shall continue 
as the Chairman of the Industrial Development Bank of 
India with effect from the date Shri S. H. Khan takes over 
as the Managing Director of Industrial Development Bank 
of India for the period upto 18th September, 1993. Shri S. S. 
Nadkarni, shall exercise the powers of a whole time Director 
in terms of the provisions of sub-section (2) of section 5 
of the Industrial Development Bank of India Act, 1964. 

[F. No. 7/13/90 BO.l]

New Delhi, the -31st January, 1992 

S.O. 606.—VVhereas on 5th November, 1969, a scheme 
of amalgamation of the Bank of Bebar Ltd. Patna, with the 
State Bank of Indiu was sanctioned by -the Central Gov¬ 
ernment in exercise of the powers conferred by and in ac¬ 
cordance with Section 45 of the Banking Regulation Act, 
1949. 

Whereas under sub-clause (ix) of clause 6 of the said 
Scheme, the State Bank of India was required to make a 
final valuation of the asscls of the Bank of Behar Ltd. which 
have been provisionally valued on the prescribed date, on 
(he expiry of twelve years from the prescribed date; 

Whereas (he State Bank of India has represented that in 
view of large number of assets involved and the recovery of 
most of, the items yet to bo realised inspilc of its efforts, 
it has not. been able to make the Jinal valuation within the 
time specified in sub-clause (ix) of clause 6 of the scheme 
of amalgamation; 

And. whereas - the Central Government afier consultation 
with the Reserve Bank of IndiH, is satisfied that a difficulty 
hgs arisen in gjvjng effect to the scheme of amalgamation 
which it is necessary to remove by extending the time within 
which the final valuation of assets is required to be made; 

Now. therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred b> 
clause 20 of the scheme of amalgamation dated 5th Novem¬ 
ber, 1969 of the Bank of Behar Ltd., Patna, W'ith tJi« fjtgtc 
Rank of India, the Central Government hereby directs that 
the State Bank of India' shall, in consultation with and with 
•he approval of the Reserve .Bank of India, value the asset* 
of the Bank of Behar Ltd,, Patna, which have not been .rea¬ 
lised and valued, within a period of twentv three years from 
,h - "rescribed date.

MINISTRY OF AGRICULTURE 
(Department of Rural Development) 

DIRECTORATE OF MARKETING AND INSPECT ION 
Faridabad, the 31st January, 1992 

S.O. 621. In exercise of the powers conferred on me under the General Grading and Marking 
Rules, 1988 and in partial modification of this office order number F.7(l 5)/73 Gen. D-ffi dated Sept’75 on 
the subject, I, O.P. Behari, Agricultural Marketing Adviser to the Government of India hereby delegate, 
in pursuance of the rules cited in column (1), authority to exercise the powers, as specified in column (2), to 
the officers of the State Government specified in colum (3), in respect of grading and marking of agri¬ 
cultural and other produce in accordance with the grade designations and the Grading and Marking Rules 
prescribed under the Agricultural Produce (Grading and Marking) Act, 1937 (l of 1937) for domestic 
market in the State of Tamil Nadu.

MINISTRY OF PETROLEUM AND NATURAL GAS 
New Delhi, the 6th February, 1992 

S.O. 624.—Whereas it appears to the Central Govern¬ 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of Petroleum from Kandla in the State of Gujarat 
to Bhatinda in the State of Punjab, via. Rajasthan and 
Haryana, pipeline(s) should be laid by Indian Oil Corpora¬ 
tion Limited. 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipelinc(s) it is necessary to acquire the right of user 
in the land described in the Schedule annexed to this noti¬ 
fication; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (11 of section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 
Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) Act, 
1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby declares 
its intention to acquire the right of user therein. 

Any person interested in the land described in the said 
schedule may within 21 days from the date on which the 
copies of (his notification, as published in the Gazette of 
India, are made available to the general public, object in 
writing to the acquisition of the right of user therein or 
laying of the pipeline under the land to Shri R. P. Kaushik, 
Tehsildar and Competent Authority, Indian Oil Corporation, 
Ltd. Kandla-Bhatinda Pipeline Project, 1158, Section-13, 
Urban Estate, Kamal, Haryana.

New Delhi, the 6th February, 1992 

S.O, 625.—Whereas it appears to the Central Govern¬ 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for tire 
transport of Petroleum from Kandla in the State of Gujarat 
to Bhatinda in the State of Punjab, via Rajasthan and 
Haryana, pipeline^) should be laid by Indian Oil Corpora¬ 
tion Limited. 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline(s) it is necessary' to acquire the right of user 
in the land described in the Schedule annexed to this noti¬ 
fication; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 


Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) Act, 
1962 (50 of 19621, the Central Government hereby declares 
its intention to acquire the right of user therein. 


Any person interested in the land described in the said 
schedule may within 21 days from the date on which the 
copies of this notification, as published in the Guzelte of 
India, are made available to the general public, object in 
writing to the acquisition of Ihc right of user therein or 
laying of the pipeline under the land to Shri R. P. Kaushik, 
Tchsildar and Competent Authority, Indian Oil Corporation, 
Ltd. Kandla-Bhatinda Pipeline Project, 1158, Sector-13, 
Urban Estate, Karnal, Haryana, 


Tehsil : Karnal

ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of Petroleum from Kanclla in the State of Gujarat 
to Bhatinda in the State of Punjab, via Rajasthan and

such pipellne(s) it is necessary to acquire the right of user

fication;

sub-section (1) of section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 
Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) Act, 
1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby declares 
its intention to acquire the right of user therein. 

Any person interested in the land described in the said 
schedule may within 21 days from the date on which the 
copies of this notification, as published in the Gazette of

New Delhi, the 6th February, 1992 

S.O. 628.—Whereas it appears to the Central Govern¬ 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of Petroleum from Kandla in the State of Gujarat 
to Bhatinda in the State of Punjab, via. Rajasthan and 
Haryana, pipeline(s) should be laid by Indian Oil Corpora¬ 
tion Limited. I 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipcline(s) it is necessary to acquire the right of user 
in the land described in the Schedule annexed to this noti¬ 
fication; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 
Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) Act, 
1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby declares 
its intention to acquire the right of user therein. 

Any person interested in the land desciibed in the said 
schedule may within 21 days from the date on which the 
copies of this notification, as published in the Gazette of 
India, are made available to the general public, object in 
writing to the acquisition of the right of user therein or 
laying of the pipeline under the land to Shri R. P. Kaushik, 
Tehsildar and Competent Authority, Indian Oil Corporation, 
Ltd. Kandla-Bhatinda Pipeline Project, 1158, Sector-13, 
Urban Estate, Karnal, Haryana.

New Delhi, the 6th February, 1992 
S.O. 629--Whereas it appears to the Central Govern¬ 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
382 C.l/92—19 


transport of Petroleum from Kandla in the State of Gujarat 
to Jihatinda in the State of Punjab, via. Rajasthan and 
Haryana, pipelined) should be laid by Indian Oil Corpora¬ 
tion Limited. 

And whereas it appears that for the purpose of laying 
such pipeline(s) it is necessary to acquire the right of user 
in the land described in the Schedule annexed to this noti¬ 
fication; 

Now, therefore, m exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (1) of section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 
Pipelines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) Act, 
1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby declares 
its intention to acquirer the right ot user therein, 

Any person interested in the land described in the said 
schedule may within 21 days from the date on which the 
copies of this notification, as published in the Gazette of 
India, are made available to the general public, object in 
writing to the acquisition of the right of user therein or 
laying of the pipeline under the land to Shri R. P. Kaushik, 
Tehsfidar and Competent Authority, Indian Oil Corporation, 
Ltd, Kandla-Bhatinda Pipeline Project, 1158, Sector-13, 
Urban Estate, Kama], Haryana.

Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government f 
hereby publishes the following award of the Central Gov¬ 
ernment Industrial Tribunal, Chandigarh as shown in the 
Annexure in the industrial dispute between the employers in 
relation to the management of Food Corporation of India 
and their workmen, which was received by the Central Gov¬ 
ernment on 23rd January, 1992.

AWARD 

Central Government vide gazette notification No. I.-42012/ 
125/87/2IB) dated 18th January, 1989 issued u/s 10(J)(d) 
of Ihe I.D. Act, 1947 referred the following dispute to this 
Tribunal for decision : 

“Whether the action of the management of Food Cor¬ 
poration of India represented through Managing 
Director, FCI, New Delhi Zonal Manager (N) New 
Delhi and Sr. Regional Manager, Chandigarh in dis¬ 
missing S-hri Jaswant Singh, watchman from service 
w.c.f, 12th March, 1986 is justified? If not then 
what other relief the workman is entitled to and 
from what date ?” 

2. Present reference was initiated by the Union and pend 
ing in this Court. However, Mr. P. K. Singla General Secre¬ 
tary of the Union who had initiated the reference by way 
of cluim statement has made statement that he has no in¬ 
structions to pursue the matter and the same may he treated 
as withdrawn. In view of the statement made by Shri P. K. 
Singla, General Secretary of the Union who had initiated 
the present reference, the present reference is dismissed as 
withdrawn and returned to the Ministry. 

Chandigarh, 

5th December, 1991.

The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour in 
exercise of the powers conferred on them by clause (d) 
of sub-section (1) and sub-section (2-A) of Section 10 of 
the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, has referred the follow¬ 
ing dispute to this Tribunal for adjudication vide Ministry's 
Order No. L-22012/481 /90HR (C. IT), dated 16-4-1991. 


Central Government vide Gazettee Notification No. L- 
42012/49/87-D. T[. B/D. IV. B, dated 20th December, 

1988 issued u/s 10(J)(d) of the I. D. Act, 1947 referred 
the following dispute to this Tribunal for decision :■— 

“Whether the action of the District manager. Food 
Corporation of India, Amritsar in terminating 
services of Shri Lakjubir Singh, Watchman in 
contravention of provision of Section 25-F is 
Justified ? If not. to what relief the workman 
concerned is entitled 7“ 

2. Present reference was initialled by the Union and 
pending in this Court. However, Mr. P. K. Singja General 
Secretory of the Union who had initiated the reference by 
way of claim statement has made statement that the work¬ 
man is not. traceable for the last 2/3 years and he has 
no instructions to pursue the matter and the same may be 
'treated as withdrawn. In view of the statement made by 
Shri P. K, Singla General Secretary of the Union who had 
initiated the present reference, the present reference is 
returned to the Ministry as withdrawn. 

Chandigarh : 5-12-1991.

Employers in relation to the management of M/s. W.C.

(M.P,). 


AWARD 

Dated, January 10th, 1992 

This is a reference made by the Central Government, 
Ministry of Labour, vide its Notification No. L-22012(157)/ 
89-IR-Coal-Il, dated 30-11-1989, for adjudication of the 
foHowing dispute :—- 

“Whether the action of the General Manager, W. C. 
Ltd., Pench Area, P.O. Parasia, Distt. Chhindwara 
in retiring Sri Mahadeo S/o Kishan, Pumo Driver, 
T. No. 574 of Bhamori colliery we.f. 1-4-85 after 
the report of the Area Medical .Board without cor¬ 
recting their mistake was justified? If not, to wfiat 
relief the workman concerned is entitled?” 

2. The workman concerned, Shri Mahadeo, was a Pump 
Driver, T. No. 574, at Bhatnori Colliery, Pench Area of 
W. C. Ltd. Me has been retired With effect from 1-4-1985. 
He is said to have attained 60 years age of superannuation. 

3. The workman says that since the management wanted 
to remove him from service (he Dy. Chief Personnel Mana¬ 
ger, Pench Area, issued a letter to the workman dated 5th 
August, 1984 and advised him to appear before the Medical 
Board for medical examination. He mentioned the date of 
bir;’h of Shri Mahadeo as 1-11-1928. The workman was sent 
to Barkui Hospital. He appeared before the Chief Medical 
Officer for med ; cal examination on 18-6-1984, He was ex- 
amifted by the Medical Board and was found fit and he 
was also found lo be 56 years of age. 

4. Having received this information the management issued 
a letter .dated 22*9-1984 slating that the workman has re.tch- 
382 GI/92—21 


11 09 


ed his age of superannuation i.e. 69 years on 31-3-1985 and 
he was retired from 1-4-1985. 

5. The workman raised objection, produced documents 
relating to his date of birth, School Leaving Certificate, 
Amravati, but to no effect. The order of his retirement is, 
therefore, illegal. He is liable to be reinstated with full 
benefits and back wages. 

6 . Management says that the reference speaks that the 
workman was retired after the report of the Area Medical 
Board without correcting the mistake. It is factually in¬ 
correct. The appropriate Government has exceeded its juris¬ 
diction. The reierence is net in confirmity with the dispute 
raised by the Union. It is, therefore, liable to be rejected 
on this count alone. 

7. As per statutory ‘B’ Form Register the age of the 
workman has been given as 50 years as on Ul-1974. The 
management would have been justified in retiring him from 
1-1 1984, but due to oversight of the dealing clerk the work¬ 
man could not be letired with effect from 1-1-1984. During 
the course of checking of the Form B Register by the Col¬ 
liery Manager the mistake was detected and the workman 
was retired from service with effect from 1-4-1985. 

8 . The issue of determination/verification of the age of 
employees was fixed and finalised at the 19th meeting of the 
JBCCl held at Calcutta on 16-1-1982. Objections were ac¬ 
cordingly invited. The letter issued by the Dy. Chief Per¬ 
sonnel Manager dated 5-8-1984 addressed to the Chief Medical 
Officer regarding medical examination was issued because the 
workman himself apptied for referring him to Medical Board 
and inadvertantly date of birth of the workman was written 
as 1-11-1928. The workman has taken the advantage of this 
mistake. 

9. Prior to this the workman had filed a Civil Suit No. 
135 A/85 which was dismissed as the workman could not 
prove that his age is 1-11-1928. The matter having already 
been decided bv a competent Court the present reference is 
barred by principles of res judicata. The workman was never 
retired on the basis of the Medical Board report dated 10th 
September, 1984. He was retired on the basis of his date 
of birth entered in Form B Register. The date of birth in 
the Area Medical Report dated 10-9-1984 is not based on 
medical evidence but on a letter dated 5-8-1984 written by 
the Dy. C.P.-M. The assessment by the Medical Board is 
not for determination of any age but only for determination 
of his medical fitness. His age was inadvertantly recorded 
as 1-11-1928 because of the clerical mistake and secondly 
the medical report also committed the same mistake. The 
Area Medical Report was never constituted for the purpose 
of assessing the age of the w'orkman. The reference is, 
therefore, liable to be rejected. 


10. In rejoinder the management has stated that the Cer¬ 
tificate submitted by the workman is not genuine. 

11. Reference was the issue in this case. 

Findings with Reasons : 

12. The workman has proved one document Ex. W/l and 
the management admilted Ex. M/1. The workman has ex¬ 
amined himself on oath bv way of affidavit. This is all the 
evidence which has been placed before me. 


13. Ex. W/l is the order of my predecessor dated 6-4-88. 
My predecessor has held that the matter of determination 
of age cannot be determined under Section 33-CI2) of the 
T.D. Act. It has. however, observed in para 3 of the order 
that the competent court can give a verdict on this point. 


i4. judgment hx. M/1 shows that on this matter the work- 
man had filed a civil suit. We are not going into the merits on 
this issue as to whether the civil court could entertain (his 
claim or not, the fact remains that this point was raised 
-before (he comment court, of law and in this civil suit the 
workman had failed to file the School Leaving Certificate. 
It follows that the School .Leaving Certificate produced by 
the workman is not a genuine document and on this count 
alone, his claim should be rejected and the version of the 
management should be accepted. That apart, Civil Court 
has specifically written the circumstances which show that 
the cteim of ffie workman.-is not just. The workman in para 
3 of his affidavit itself admitted that the Medical Board vide 



II 10

Us Report dated 18-6-84 ha, shown the woiFman lo U nt 
for service One can he very much lit for work at the age 
of fiO teats in these day,. Thus the recording of age of the 
workman in form B Regislei cannot be questioned on the 
grounds raised by the wodknian, particularly when the matter 
has been decided b\ ;i competent Court of law with all the 
reasoning given therm. 

15. The workman is. therefore, not entitled to the tel.ef 
as claimed. The reference is accordingly answered as 
follows :—. 

"file action of the General Manager, W. C. l td., Pench 
Area. P.O. Eurasia, Distl. Chhindwara in retiring 
Sri Mahadeo S/o Kishnn, Pump Driver, T, No. 574 
of Bhamori Colliery w.c.f. 1-4-1985 after the report 
of the i-rea Medical Board without correcting their 
mistake was justified. He is not entitled to any 
relief. No order as lo costs,

Dated, (he 14th January, 1992 
AWARD 

The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour in 
exercise of the powers conferred on them by clause td) of 
sub-section (1) and sub-section f2A) of Section 10 of the 
Industrial Disputes Act. 1947 has referred the following dis¬ 
pute to this Tribunal for adjudication vide Ministry's Order 


F792/PH\LGL>.A 3, 1913 [Pari U~-Su\ 3(.iij] 

” ■> I -:V.012< 185>/90-!RfC.II) dated the 14th November, 
l‘*90. 

SCHEDULE 

“Whether the action of Ihe management of Kuardi (R) 
Colliery under Salgram Area of M/s. ECU, in dis¬ 
missing Md. Kifiam Khan, Pit Clerk, vide dismissal 
older No. SAT/GM/84/162 dated 25-12-1984 is 
justified ? If nm, to what relief the workman is 
entitled ?’’ 

2. The case of the union in brief is that Md. Kalam Khan 
the concerned workman was a worker of Kuardi Colhefy 
under Eastern (. outfields l.tu. The workman served the colliery 
for over 12 years to the entire satisfaction of the manage¬ 
ment and there was no occasion for any complaint against 
his performance and conduct while he wus in service of the 
company. 

In August, 1984 the concerned workman was served with 
a chargesheet with the allegation that he was securing vari¬ 
ous contract jobs of tire colliery by setting up one man 
named Rahmat Khan. There was further allegation that he 
presented inilated bill for such contract work. That charge- 
sheet is dated 21-8-84. On 16-10-84 the workman submilfed 
written reply but the authority was not satisfied with the 
explanation given by him. So the management started a 
domestic enquiry against the workman and in that domestic 
enquiry the concerned workman was found guilty for both 
the counts of the charges. On the result of the domestic 
enquiry ihe workman was dismissed from service w.e.f. 
25-12-84. 

3. The workman raised dispute through union. Attempts 
of conciliation failed. The matter was sent to the Minlslry 
of Labour, Government o( India and ultimately the MiiuTJfty 
of Labour has referred the dispute to this Tribunal tor 
adjudication. 

4. The management has filed written objection contending 
inler-alin that the concerned workman set up one man named 
Rahront Khan and secured various contract work in different 
collieries in his name and thereby he has defrauded the com¬ 
pany by suppressing the fact that the works were actually- 
executed by him. The domestic enquiry was properly and 
fairly held. He has been rightly dismissed from service by 
the mamtgemem on Ihe result of the domestic enquiry. Hie 
management has also denied all the material averments made 
by the union in its written statement. 

5. In this case ihe union challenged the validity of the 

domestic enquiry. It was also contended that the domestic 
enquiry was not pioperiy and fairly held and the pnnciples 
of natural justice were violated in holding the domestic 
enquiry. This point came up for hearing on 19-12-91. Sri 
J. D. Lai the learned Advocate for the union during the 
hearing of the case submitted that the “Union does not chal¬ 
lenge the vuldity and fairness of the enquiry any more. 

I he Court may come to a finding that the principles of 
natural justice were not violated in this case in holding the 
domesilc enquiry’". On such submission made by Sri J. D. 
I.al this Court has come to a finding that the domestic enquiry 
was properlv and fairly held and no principle of natural 
justice was violated in this case as the Court finds that the 
workman properly participated in Ihe domestic cnqniry, 

6 . As the Court has found that the domestic enquiry was 
properly and fairly held. Ihe scope of the Tribunal is now 
limited. Sri J. D. l.al. learned Advocate for the workman 
has urged that this Tribunal is not bound by the findings of 
the learned Enquiry Officer. The Tribunal can re-ftpprise the 
entire evidence and nmy come to a different finding and on 
this- point he has relied on the case reported In S.C.L.J. Vol. 
10 page 159, The Tribunal can alter or reduce the punish¬ 
ment if after re apprisal of the evidence the Tribunal also 
finds the workman guiliv. Sri P. K. Das the learned Advo¬ 
cate for the management with his usual fairness submits that 
there is nothing to sav against the legal propositions as sub¬ 
mitted by Sri J. O. l.al. 

So it is now the admiited position that the Tribunal can 
re-appri.se the entire evidence and may come to an independent

2 ' 1 , I 9!13/f3KiJ^ 3,19 I 3 


nil 


conclusion disagreeing with the conclusion already arrived 
at by the Id. Enquiry Officer. 

6 . In this case the following charges (.Ext. M-D wcic 
framed against the concerned workman which read as 
follows : 

“CHARUESHEET” 

No. CS/84/2828 Dated : 21-8-1984. 

Shri Kalam Khan, 

Pit Clerk, 

Knardi Colliery. 

It has transpired that yuu set up one man named Rahmat 
khan and secured various contract work in different col¬ 
lieries particulars of which are mentioned below in his name 
and have defrauded that company by suppressing the actual 
met that the said contract jobs had actually taken up and 
executed by you. 

Your aforesaid action constitutes fraud and dishonesty in 
connection with the company’s business and the company had 
to face the risk of issuing cheque No. 304165 oil State Bank 
of India, Axunsol, cm 2.9-6-84 for Rs. 92,976 towards the 
pavment of the dues of an inflated bill submitted or caused 
to have been sutnii'tcd by you, the payment of which, how¬ 
ever, had been stopped whtn the actual fact came to the 
notice qf the management. By aforesaid action, you have 
made yourself liable for committing misconduct, as per Para 
l7(]Xn) of the certified standing order applicable to you 
which tends hs under :— 

“Para 17(1 )a>—Theft, fraud or dishonesty in connection 
with the employer's business or property, disclosure 
of any confidential or important information or any 
information relating to the employer’s affairs or busi¬ 
ness or unauthorised or dishonest removal of any 
paper, document or books of the company.” 

Particulars of Contract:— 

1. Depeat Order No. SAT/GM/AE(C)/83I1986, dated 
lOt 15-3*83 for the supply of 1st Class Kiln Burnt Brick— 
2.5 lacs to Kuardi. 

2. Work Order No. 0306803 dated 26-4-84 for the supply 
of 1st Class Kiln Burnt Ericks as follows:— 

Kuardi (R-— 2 lacs. 

Satgram <R)—6 lacs. 

Rutibati (R)—7 lacs. 

You are. hereby directed to explain in writing within 
48 hours from the date of receipt of this chargesheef as 
to why disciplinary action shall not be taken against yon 
failing which the Management may take further action as 
It deems flit. 

Sd./- Illegible, 
Agent 

Kuardi/Tirat Collieries.” 

On the first count of the charge I find that there is allega¬ 
tion that being a worker of the Company, Md. Kalam Khan 
set up one Rahmat Khan and secured several contract jobs 
and he actually executed the said contract jobs 
I have carefully gone through the entire evidence adduced 
bv the management during domestic enquiry. Considering the 
entire evidence on record and the facts and circumstances 
f hare no hesitation to agree with the learned Enquiry Officer 
that in fact Md. Kalam Khan set up one Rahmat Khan 
and procured some contract jobs of the company and he 
executed the same. 

As regatds the second count of charge I find that there is 
allegation of fraud, ft has been alleged during domestic 
enquiry tl'.at Md. Kalam Khan made attempts to get the 
inflated fc'H passed. Sri J. D. La! the learned Advocate for 
the workman has taken me through the statements made by 
l\fW-3. MW- 4 , MW-5 and MW-6. It appears from their state¬ 
ments that they knew the matter from one Sri K, K Gupta, 
Senior Accounts Officer. But that Senior Accounts Officer has 
not been examined. Sri Cat has urged before me that the find¬ 
ings of the Enquiiy Office! is not supported by legal evidence. 


He has urged before me that the Tribunal or the Enquiry 
Officer cannot come to H finding merely on conjectures and 
on this point he lias relied on a case reported in A.ER, 1972 
(S.C.J page 2535. 

Considciing the entire evidence’ adduced in the domestic 
enquiry and all the inch and circumstances of the present 
case I find that there is no satisfactory evidence in the record 
to show that the present workman actually made attempts 
to defraud the company. I am unable to agree with the 
findings of the learned Enquiry Officer on the second count 
of charge. 

7. So i find that Md. Kalam Khan the concerned workman, 

sc; lip one Kahmat Khan and procured some contract jobs 
of the company and executed the same. Sri J. D. Lai has 
urged before me that such act of the workman docs not 

amount to dishonesty. He has cited before me the case 

reported in Lab. l.C. 1985 page 729 wherein it has been 

held ihat ‘.he Court cannot fish out dishonesty and he has 

also cited bclore me the cane reported in Lab. l.C. 1983 page 
f909 wherein it has been held thal misconduct must be 
ejiumei.ued in he Standing Order of the company. He has 
urged, before me that In Clause 17( 1 >(a) ol the Standing Order 
wh oil law been referred in the chargeshcet there is no men¬ 
tion of any act as dishonesty. He has urged before me that 
the present alleged act of the workman cannot be treated 
as dishonesty. Sri 1*. K. Das, the learned Advocate lor the 
management has urged before me that dishonesty is a veiy 
wqJc term which has been mentioned in the present standing 
outer. Considering all the facts and circumstances I am 
in agreement with [lie learned Lawyer for llie management. 
I find that the alleged act of the workman amonls to some 
soil of dishonesty which is covered by Section 17(1) of the 
Standing Order. So 1 find that the learned Enquiry Officer 
rightly found the concerned workman guilty on ihe count 
of charge of dishonesty. 

8 . Now we shall see whether the punishment awarded in 
this case is proportionate with the alleged offence. Sri P. K. 
Das the learned Advocate for the management has urged 
before me that reinstatement is not a matter of natural rule. 
He has cited before me the eases reported in 1990 Lab. l.C, 
page 844, 1981 Lab. l.C. p. 1292, AIR 1980 (SC) page 1896, 
1970 Lab. l.C, puge 1166 and by placing those cases he has 
urged bclore me that in thi«s case the dismissal of the con¬ 
cerned workman is quite proportionate. On the other hand 
Sri J. D. 1 al the learned Advocate for the union has urged 
before me that such act docs not warrant dismissal from 
service which is the extreme punishment. The Hon’ble 
Supreme C ourt has held that capital punishment shall be 
imposed in a rare of the rarest cases, in these hard days 
dismissal from service is worse than capital punishment. 

So considering all the tacLs and circumstances of the pre¬ 
sent case I find that the dismissal from service in the instant 
cure is a disproportionate punishment in view of the nature 
of the offence committed by the present workman. I find 
that in a case like the present one it will meet the ends of 
justice if the entire back wages of Ihe concerned woikman 
is forlieled. 

9. In the result 1 find that dismissal of Md. Kalam Khan 
the concerned workman w.c.f. 25-12-84 was not justified. 
Md. Kalam Khan shall be reinstated in service within two 
months fium the date of publication of the award without 
any back wages. If the management fails to reinslate him 
within the said stipulated period, Md. Kalam Khan shall 
stand reinstated from the dale of expiry of that period of 
two monihs and he shall get his wageA from that date. The 
entire back wages of Md Kalam Khan are foriieted as 
punishmeiit. 

1 ni>. is my award.

bunal:— 

Whether the action of management of State Bank of

K.' D. Sharma, Ex-clerk-e urn-cashier w.e.f. 18-6-91 
and denial of regular employment on compassion¬ 
ate ground was justified 7 If not, to what relief is 
the concerned workman entitled? 

2. In the present case dates 21 12-90, 3U-1-91, 18-3-91, 
7-5-91, 15-7-91 were given to the Union/workman to file 
rejoinder. On 4-9-91, one Sri K. N. Sonc, appeared in Ihe 
case and submitted before the Tribunal that he had not 
received any letter of authority in his favour either front 
the workman or from the Union.. The Tribunal vide its 
order dt. 4-9-91 closed the right of the Union/workmnn to

further orders on 9-12-91. On 9-12-91 again none appeared 
for the Union/workman. Sri V. B, Mathur, appeared for 
the management bank. No affidavit evidence was filed on 
9-2-91, ft therefore appears from the conduct of the 
Union/workman that neither the Union nor the workman 
is interested in prosecuting the (rase any more. Therefore, a 
no claim award is given against the U'nion/workman. 

3. Reference is answered accordingly,

2 . Ihe industrial dispute on behalf of the workman has 
been raised by All India State Bank of Patiala Staff Fede¬ 
ration, Chandigarh (hereinafter referred to as Union for the 
sake of bmvity). 

3. The case of the Union is that the workman Sri Vijay 
Kumar Mittal was promoted as Head Cashier Category B 1 
by the management from the date he took over charge at 
Bulandshahar, The Union alleges that the workman jo ned 
Bulandsbahar on 16-1-1989, At that time one clerk and 5 
clcrk-cum-car,hiei were posted in the said branch. Later 011 

‘one clerk-cum-cashier was transferred to Secunderabad 
whereupon Sri S. D. Shtfrma joined in his place as clerk- 
cum-cashier, The above strength of staff was in addition to 
the workman. In terms of V Bipartite Settlement the 
workman was entitled to special allowance of Rs. 282 per 
month as Head Cashier Category B and he was paid special 
allowance at the said rate from January, 1989 to July. 1990. 
However, the Regional Manager, vide his letter dt. 24-7-90, 
abruptly ordered the reduction of special allowance from 
R.s. 282 per month to Rs. 224 per month and directed the 
branch manager Bulandsliahar to effect recovery of the 
excess- -.pcml allowance paid to h ut. Ihe Union alleges 
that tin- said order is illegal and unjustified inasmuch as 
the said order resulted in change of service conditions of 



1113 


[«tnr It— v* 3 (ii)] ' ^ tt ww . TO* 


itic workman without following the procedure laid down 
r idet sec. 9A of the I.D, Act. 1947. Moreover, the para 
relating to payment of special allowance to head cashier 
category B as contained in the Bipartite Settlement has been 
wrongly boen interpreted by the management. fhe Union 
has, therefore, prayed that the order diretcing the manage 
ment to pay special allowance to the workman at the rate 
of R',. 28ft per month with further order to pay arrears b; 
passed against the management. 

4. The case proceeded ex parte against the management, 
fhe Union has proved its case by means of .the affidavit of 
Sri M. C, Khurana, Vice President, of the said Union. 

5. It is, therefore, held that the action of the manage¬ 
ment of the State Bank of Patiala in reducing the special 
allowance from Rs. 282 per month to ks. 224 per month 
of the workman is held as illegal and unjustified. 

6. Consequently, the workman is held entitled to receive 
special allowance at the rate of Rs. 282 per month so long 
as he holds the charge of cashier category B and is further 
held cnt’tlcd to recover arrears of special allowance. 

7. Reference is answered accordingly.

chargesheet'dt, 24-1-83.

6 . On 8-2-90, lhe following preliminary issue was framed 
in this case— 

Whether the departmental inquiry was not conducted 
fairly and properly by the management as alleged 
by the workman. 

On the said issue the workman examined himself and the 
management examined Sri Arvind Svvarup Tandon an offi¬ 
cer ot the Bank, posted in the Zonal Ollice at Lucknow, 
The management also tiled the documents relating to the 
domestic inquiry and some documents summoned by the 
workman. 

7 On 11-4-91, Sri B. D. Tewari, the authorised represen¬ 
tative lor the workman under instructions from the workman 
suomitted before the tribunal that the workman did not 
pi ess the preliminary issue framed on 8-2-90. It was also 
submitted by Sri Tewari that the workman would simply 
argue the case on the point of punishment and would seek 
to invoke the jurisdiction of the Tribunal under Sec. 11-A 
ot (lie Industrial Disputes Act, 1947. 

8. Both the ’rides have filed written atgumeuts, little 
knowing that the above statement had been made by Sri 
'I cwari on 11-4-91 under the instructions from the workman. 
l,i his written arguments, the workman has dealt with the 
points that the chargesheet is a nullity; that both the Pre¬ 
senting Officer and Enuiry Officer acted as prosecutor "and that 
tli finding given by the B.O. are perverse. Similarly Sri 
Mahesh Chandra authorised representative for the manage¬ 
ment in his written arguments has also touched above the 
points that the inquiry against the workman wa» conducted 
1 airly and properly and that the finding given by the E.O. 
are not perverse. 

9. Since there is a statement by Sri B. O. Tewari, the 
authorised representative for the workman under the in¬ 
struction, from the workman on 11-4-91 to which refer¬ 
ence has been made by me above, 1 will not be touching 
these points. I shall be confining myself only to tho point 
whether or not it is a fit case for exercise of powers by the 
Industrial Tribunal under sec. 11-A of the LD. Act. 

10. The admitted position is that the workman had taken 
■an advance amounting to Rs. 4500 from the bank on the 
pretext of attending bilateral discussions on 19th and 20th 
December, 1982 and on I4th and 15th January, 1982 at 
Bombay and Bhuvaneshwar respectively when in fact xa, 
such meetings were culled at the said two places on the 
said dates. 

11. It has been contended by the workman that the grain 
of advance by the bank to him towards T.A. constitute 
merely a debt against him and the said debt got cleared off 
when the amount of Rs. 4500 was recovered from Jvs salary 
for the months of June, July and August, 1982. After the 
recovery of the amount, the bank was not legally entitled 
to initiate disciplinary proceedings against him. 

12. In the above contention of the workman, I find no 
lT’tce. The management in their written statemet have ad¬ 
mitted this much that for attending bilateral discussions/ 
meetings the hank has been permitting the representatives of 
the Association of the workers at banks expenses. In the 
instant case the workman took an advance for a specific 
purpose and not by way of loan from the bank. Therefore, 
the amount taken by him as an advance cannot be treated 
as a loan. From the findings it is clear that he did not 
voluntarily returned the amount of the advance taken by 
h m by the bank. Ralher from the order dt. 30-11-83, of 
the Disciplinary Authority it will be evident that the 
amount was recovered from his salary and allowances and 
bonus payable to him during the period July, 1982 to April, 
J9R3 after he hnd been repeatedly called upon to make 
good the amount of the bank. 

1.3. Another point raised by the workman is that the facts 
uf (he c*.e.c does not amount to misappropriation of the 
advance taken by him from lhe bank and eorsequenlly it 
does not amount to gross misconduct tinder para 521(4)tj) 
of SnstTy Award, which defines the term Gross Misconduct. 



1115 


[ITr<T II--#* 3 (ii j] *rr<3_ff ’-W* ; ^'r 


14 In this contention also 1 find no force. It is the ad¬ 
mitted position of the parties that the two amounts were 
taken by way of advance for specific purpose, the purpose 
being to attend the bilateral discussions at Bombay and 
Bhubaneshwar. It has been find during the inquiry that 
the advance taken on two occasion was taken by the work¬ 
man on false grounds. This fact is almost admitted by the 
workman himself in his claim statement when he says that 
no such bilateral discussions had taken place at the said 
two places on the said two dates. It means that the 
amounts of advance were not utilisefl/appiopirated for the 
purpose for which they were taken. When such is a situa¬ 
tion it will be presumed that the amounts so taken by the 
workman were misappropriated. Ordinarily the term ‘MIS¬ 
APPROPRIATION’ means appropriation of the amount for 
a purposes other than the purpose for which it has been 
taken. So I fully agree with the findings of the E.O. as 
accepted by the Disciplinary authority that the act of the 
workman amounts to Misappropriation of money. 

15. The question is whether it amount to gross mis 
conduct as referred to in para 521(4)(j) of Sastry Award oi 
not. In support of his contention that such an act does 
not constitute gross misconduct under para 52(14)Cj) of the 
Sastry Award the workman has relied on the following two 
rulings. 


22,1992/Tr?iT=r 3,1913 

this statement does not appear to be wholly correct when 
seen m the light of what was found by the Drsciplmary 
Authority while passing his order dt. 30-11-83. The Dis¬ 
ciplinary Authority has clearly observed that ihe amounts 
obtained by the workman were recovered from his salary 
■and allowances and bonus payable to him during the period 
July, 1982 to April, 1983 after he had been repeatedly call¬ 
ed upon in vain to make good the amount of the bank. 
So all this does not constitute any extenuating circumstances 
in his favour. 

19. No doubt the workman deserves a deterrent punish¬ 
ment but we must not forget the humane aspect of the 
matter. No only he but his family members for no fault 
of theirs must have undergone a lot of mental agony, during 
this long period for over 7 years. In these days it is very 
difficult to get an employment. We have seen above the 
amount has already been recovered From his salary and 
bonus. I am therefore of the view that in the circumstances 
it would be a sufficient punishment if he is ordeited to be 
rerun - :;ted in service wilh 50 per cent of his back wfiges with 
stoppage of 3 annual graded increments permanently. The 
period of suspension will be treated as on duty but in res¬ 
pect of the period of suspension he will not get njore than 
what he had already got by way of subsistence allowance 
T. invoke mv powers under Sec. 11-A of the Industrial Dis¬ 
putes Act, 1947, accordingly. 


1. A. L. Kalra Versus Project & Equipment Corpora 

tiph l td., (1984) 3 SCC 316. 

2. Gopal Krishna Prabhu Versus Central Bank 1991 

SC 532 (Kerala,. 

I have gone through these rulings and find that both are 
distinguishable on facts. In the first case, the Hon’ble 
Supreme Court held ihat the acts of misconduct must be 
precised and specifically stated in rules (or Standing Orders) 
and they cannot be left to interpreted expost facto by tha 
management. These observations were made by the 
Hon’ble Supreme Court in a case where in Rules (or Stand 
ing Orders) misconduct hat; not been specifically stated. 

16. In the second case the Hon’ble High Court held that 
what in a given context would constitute conduct prejudi¬ 
cial to the interest of the bank to be treated as misconduct 
would expose an area not amenable to objective evalua¬ 
tion. If that be the position such conduct cannot be treat¬ 
ed as misconduct. However in the instant case, there is 
no ambiguity about the charge and the act of the workman. 
Further the act which has been treated as misconduct by 
the management is not such as is not amenable to objec¬ 
tive evaluation. His act is fully amenable to objective 
evaluation His case fully false within the ambit of ths 
term gross misconduct as referred to in para 521(4)(j) of 
Sastry Award. 

17. Lastly, comes a question of quantum of punishment. 
In the claim statement, the workman has described himself 
as Assistant General Secretary of the State Bank of IndU 
Staff Association, at the relevant time. It means that he 
was occupying a fairly important position in (he Association 
of Award Staff. In his capacity he would be presumed to 
be looking after the interest of the award staff deligently 
in matters relating to their services conditions and othei 
allied matters arising out of the same. Being a senior most 
office bearer of the State Bank of India Staff Association it 
wc>i expected of him to set a goal example for others. But 
if such a workman himself voilates rules and regulations 
considering himself to be above the administration, he 
would surely be setting up a bad example for the members 
of the staff association. The other members can also th : nk 
that if the management cannot take any disciplinary action 
astainst such office bearer of the staff association what the 
management could do of them if they also flaught the Rules 
etc., regulating their service matters. So the case like the 
present, the gravity of the gross misconduct increased and 
it calls for a deterrent punishment. 

18. It has.been pleaded by the workman that the amount 
of advance was recovered by the management from his 
salary from the months of June, July and August. 1982 but 


20. Hence, the order of dismissal from srevioe of the 
workman passed by the management of State 8aok of 
India is set aside and the management is directed io rein¬ 
state the workman in service with 50 per cent of! hits back 
wages with the stoppage of 3 annual graded increments per¬ 
manently. The workman will also be entitled for all con¬ 
sequential benefits. The period of suspension of the work¬ 
man will be treated as on duty in respect of the stud period 
he will not get more than what he had already got by way 
of subsistance Allowance. 

21. Reference is answered accordingly.

(No- L-120121303189-IR (B-3) ] 
S. C. SITARMA, D(sk Officer 

ANNEXURE 

BEFORE SHRI ARJAN DEV, PRESIDING 
CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL 
CUM-LABOUR COURT, PANDU NAGAR, 

Industrial Dispute No. 94 of 199 
Tn the matter of dispute between : 

BETWEEN

I OFFICER,

1116 1HL GAZiirt Or INDIA: IXiJRUARY 22, 1692/i'HALGUNA 3, 19 13 [PA?r 11 -Sec. 3(i i)] 

„ ' ^ r , - rrUrrtOn ..,-^ - ! ssr --- 22.1»- ■'* . —— 

Stale Hank Worker's Organisation 
2, Naveen Market Parade, 

Kanpur. 

AND 


The Regional Manager, 

Stare Bank of India, 

Region No. 1, 

Midi Road, Kanpur. 

AWARD 

1. The Central Government, Minixliy of Labour, side 
its notification No. L-12012 '303/89-lRifi-3| dated 6-4-1990, 
has referred the following dispute for adjudication to this 
Tribunal :—- 

■‘Whether the action of the State Bank of India, Kanpur 
in relation to their Moosa Nagar Branch in ter¬ 
minating the services of Shri Faquir Chnnd 
Agrawnl. Canteen Boy w.e.f, 12-8-1987 is justified ? 
II not, to what relief the workman is entitled to?’’ 

2. The industrial dispute on pchalf of workman Shri Faquir 
Chand Agrawal has been raised by the State Bank Workers '■ 
Organisation, Kanpur (hereinafter referred to as Union for 
the sake of brevity). 

3. The case of the Union is that the workman was 
appointed as a Canteen Boy on 16-6-1979, at Moosanagar 
Branch of the bank on a .salat y of Rs. 150 per month. 
Besides performing the duties of Canteen Boy the manage¬ 
ment took from him also the work of peon massenger and 
Daftari. From 13-8-1987 to 16-8-1987, the workman 
remained on leave but when on 17-K-19K7, he went to 
office to join his duty he was not allowed to join duty by 
the manager orally. Later on the manager Moosa Nagar 
Branch assured him that he would be given job on 30-9-87, 
but the said assurance was not honoured. The Union 
alleges violation of the provisions of Section 25N T.D. Act 
and also adoption of Unfair Labour Practice by the manage¬ 
ment with regard to the workman The Union has, there¬ 
fore, prayed for the reinstatement of the workman with 
full back wages as admissible to class IV employees of the 
bank. 

4. The case is contested by the bank. The management 
plead thaL the claimuni was engaged by the Local Imple¬ 
mentation Committee which is an mdependant body of 
Staff of Moosa Nagar Branch of lhc bank on a consolidated 
salary of Rs'. 150 per month. Lhc said salary Used to be 
paid by lhc said committee to the workman and not by 
the bank. According to the management the cunteen R 
run exclusively for the welfare of staff members on no 
profit no loss basis by the committee. the canteen Is 
neither ownod nor controlled by the bank management 
'except at a few identified bran.dics/banks, Moosa Nagar 
Brunch is not amongst such identified branches of the bank. 
Under the welfare scheme subsidiary is paid by the hunk 
to the committee v'hich meets icst of the expenses of the 
canteen through its staff members. The management further 
plead that the commitlce is not an industry and conse¬ 
quently the clniment is not a workman within the meaning 
of the provisions of ID. Act. The claimcnt was neither 
paid uny wages by the bank nor tne benefits which are 
available to the class IV employees of the bank were avail¬ 
able to him. In fact there was no relationship Of employer 
and employee between the claimcnt and the bank. Lastly, 
it is pleaded by the management that similar disputes were 
raised in LCA Case Nos. 151 of 1987 to 157 of 1987 and 
160 of 1987 to 170 of 1987 by lhc various canteen boys 
against the bank but the said cases were dismissed by this 
very court by means of a detailed order dated 27-4-1988, 

5. In support of his case, the Union has examined oral 

as well ns documentary evidence. The ora] evidence con¬ 
sists of the statement of the workman. 

6 . I have gone through the evidence on record ami the 

relevant provisions of the I.D. Act and find that the Union 
has no case at all. This very question whether a Canteen 
Boy is a workman within the meaning pf section 2(s) I.D. 

Act came up for consideration before this court in LCA 

cases referred to by tb c management in their written 


statement. After considering the evidence and some staff 
circulars issued by the State Bank of India, it was held by 
this court that a Canteen Boy is not a WORKMAN within 
lire meaning of Section 2(s) I.D. Act. I see no reason 
10 differ with rny said view. Exception only exists iu res 
pect of such Cunteen Boys as have been working in certain 
identified branches named in the Schedule annexed to the 
agieement between the Stale Bank of India and its employees 
regarding Canteen Employees dated 31st October, 1977. 
Admittedly Sri Faquir Chand Agrawal had been working as 
a Canteen Boy in the canieen run by Locul Implementation 
Committee as Moosa Nagar Branch. The name of this 
branch docs not appear in the li>t of branches and office < 
given in [he schedule, 

7. In his cross examination, the workman has admitted 
the existence of Local Implementation Committee at Moosa 
Nagar Branch of the Bank. He also admits that the said 
committee runs a canteen in the said branch. The canteen 
boys are paid wages through cheques signed by the Secre¬ 
tary and the President of the Committee. He has futther 
admitted (hat as a canteen boy he was not enjoying bene¬ 
fits similar to those enjoyed by the award stall' nor he was 
getting salary equal to that of sub staff. From para 10 
of my order in the LCA case it appear that only subsidy 
is paid by the bank to tills committee for running the 
canieen. 

8 . In the above circumstances I hold that as a canteen 
boy Sri Faquir Chand Agrawal was not an employee of 
the bank but was an employee of the Local Implementa¬ 
tion Committee. As such he was not a workman within 
tile meaning of Section 2($) I.D. Act. 

9. The Union has further come out with the case thar 
ihe case of Sri Faquir Chand Agrawal has also worked as 
peon massenger and duflari. In my view this part of the 
Union's case cannot be considered in view of the reference 
order in-as-imich as in the reference order Sri Agrawal 
has bee referred to as Canteen Boy only. Even otherwise 
I find no substance in the case of the Union. 

10. In para 5 of his affidavit Sri Agrawal has deposed 
that during the neriod 26-8-1980 to 10-9-1983 he had 
worked for total number of 71 days as a temporary 
messenger and in support of it he has filed the photo stat 
copy of the certificate dated 28- 5-1986 issued by the Branch 
Manger, Moosa Nagar Branch. There is no evidence in 
rebuttal. So the period during which he had worked as 
a temporary messanger he would be deemed a workman 
within the meaning of Section 2fs) I.D. Act. But this is 
not sufficient to grant relief to Sri Agrawal. I have not 
been shown as to how in view of his having worked for 
71 days as temporary messenger during the period of 3 
years he became entitled to anv relief. The authorised 
representative for the Union Sri D. N, Tewari, has not been 
able to show any violation of any provisions of the I.D. 
Act bv the management bank. So even on this count the 
Union’s case fails. 

11. Held that the question of termination of services of 
Sri Faquir Chand Agrawal Canteen Boy bv the manage¬ 
ment of State Bank of India, in relation to Its Moosa Nagar 
Branch does not arise at all as Sri Agrawal was not an 
employee of the bank as a canteen boy. 

12. Consequently the Union is held entitled to no relief. 

13. Reference is answered accordingly. 

ARJAN DEV, Presiding Officer,

APPEARANCES;

ST ATE : West Bengal. INDUSTRY : Coal. 

AWARD 

On the allegations of premature, illegal and unjustified ter¬ 
minated of service by way of superannuation of 1. Sobrati 
Mia of Chinakuri Mine No. Ill and 2. Lai Mohammad, 
Mechanical Foreman of Chinakuri Mine No. 1 under Eastern 
Coalfields Ltd., with effect from July 1, 1985, the following 
dispute :— 

(i) “Whether the action of the Management of Chinak¬ 
uri Mine No, III of M|s. E. C, Lid., and 

(ii) Whether the action of the Management of Chinakuri 

Mine No. I of M|s. E. C. Ltd., in not accepting 
the age of Shri Sobrati Mia, Stone Cutter Chinakuri 
Mine No, III and Shri Lai Mohammad, Mechanical 
Foreman, Chinakuri Mine No. J us recorded in the 
Identity Card Issued by the Erstwhile Employer, 
viz. M|s, Bengal Coal Co, Lid., and terminating 
their services w.e.f. 1-7-1985 on the gtound of attain¬ 
ing age of superannuation is justified 7 If not, to 
what relief the workmen arc entitled ?” 

Alter conciliation, was referred for adjudication to 
this Tribunal by the appropriate government by 
Order No 1. |9012(100)|86-D.1V(B) dated 23rd April, 
1987. 


2. From a reference to the order sheet arid records of this 
piocctding’j, it will appear that initially mere was no appea- 
luitce entered by ’and on behalf of the employees, even though 
services was duly alfcctcd and thereafter, one Shri P. K. 
Goswumi appeared for some days for the employees concern¬ 
ed, but ultimately, neither the said Shri Goswami nor the 
employees ellecuvely took part in the proceedings or appttrr- 
eu in support of their contentions, although pleadings of 
the patties were duly completed, 

3. It was the case of the employees represented by West 
Bengal Elian Mazdoor bungli (U.T.l.C.) of Chinakuri Col¬ 
liery thereinafter reieircd to as the said Union) that the

lion m the Coal Industry.

6 . The Employers in their written statement, have claimed

Form viz. B F’orm Register, wherein ihe particulars of all

1. The Central Government, Ministry of Labour vide 
its notification No. E-I20T2) JJ0I89-D-2- A dated 
16-4-90 has referred the following dispute for adjudication to 
this Tribunal :— 

Whether the Relgonul Manager, Bank of India, T.ucknow 
was justified in imposing penalty of stoppage of one 
increment permanently to Sri Rattan Kumar Cashier 
IncHnrge of Madion Branch? If not, to what relief 
the workman is entitled ? 

2. T he Industrial Dispute on behalf of the workman Sri 
Rattan Kumar has been raised by Bank of India Staff Union, 
Kanpur. 

3. The admitted facts arc that the workman while he vva', 
posted us clerk cum cashier nt Madion Branch was served 
with chnrgesheet dated 25-2-8.1 issued by Regional Manager, 
Lucknow Region. One Sri K. D. Rawat. posted as sub staff 
at the said branch was also similarly charged, The charge- 
sheet read as follows.— 

Dating the month of April 1981 yon alongvriib Sri K, D. 
Rawat Staff Subordinate Madion Branch demanded 
a bride of Rupees One thousand five hundred

[vnrj [I--«T .1 (ii ) 1 'Tran rrsrw. fi -M »»3, is 13 


(Rb. 1500.) from Sri Rujendra Erased Yadav who 
was working at Matlion Brunch us a Waterboy on 
d'aii> wages, with an occurrence to get Sri Yadav 
appointed in the Bunks service as a Badlee Sepoy. 
Both of you also stipulated that out of the aforesaid, 
amount of Rs. 1500 an amount of Rs. 500 (Rupees 
hive Hundred Only) be paid to you immediately 
■and Ihe balance of Rs. 1,000 may be paid after he 
gets a job as a Badlee Sepoy in the bank. Accord¬ 
ingly Sri Yadav gave Rs. 500 on 26-4-81 to Sri 
Haw at in vour presence and he accepted the same as 
a bribe, Your aforesaid act of demanding and 
accepting bribe amount to gross misconduct within 
the meaning of clause 14.5(k) of the First Bipartite 
Settlement dated 19-10-66. 

Ihe Branch. Manager appointed Sii D, Ghosh Roy, Dy, 
Chief Officer, Credit Department, Zonal Office, Lucknow, as 
l-.nquiry Officer. He held inquiry into [he said charges against 
(he said two employees of the bank on 17-5-83, and 19-5-83. 
On these two dates, however, he did not record any evidence. 
Thei cutter, the Regional Manager, appointed Sri P. Kumar, 
Industrial Relation Officer, Central Office, Bombay as En- 
Ljuii-y Officer, He completed inquij# and vide his finding 
dated 2-9-83. he held the charge as proved againtit the 
workman. The Regional Manager who happened to be the 
disciplinary authority 'accepted the finding given by (he E. O. 
and issued a notice to the workman to .,how cause why 
punishment of stoppage of 4 increments permanently be not 
awarded to him. After hearing the workman arid his defence 
representative, the disciplinary authority, vide his order dt, 
27-12-83, Modified the said notice and uwarded to the 
workman the punishment of stoppage of J increments per¬ 
manently. Against the order of punishment, the workman 
liled an appeal which was partly allowed by the Appellate 
Authority (Zonal Manager <J. P. Zone) vide his order dl. 
26-10-84. He substituted the punishment awarded by the 
Disciplinary Authority by a punishment of stoppage of one 
increment to the workman permanently. 

4, 'I he Union hr is assailed the order oi punishment on 
a number of ground’-. The Union alleges that the bunk has 
not displayed on its notice board a notice to the effect that 
the Chairman and Managing Director of the Bunk has been 
empowered to appoint Regional Managers of the bank as 
Disciplinary Authority by the Board of Directors of the

of Directors whercunder the Disciplinary Authority hat, been 
authorised to appoint Enquiry Officer. The Regional 
Manager, J. Lie know, even if appointed as Disciplinary Autho¬ 
rity was not empowered to appoint Sri P. Kumar as Enquiry 
Officer as the said officer was posted outside his region. 

5. Next it is alleged by the Union that during the course 
of inquiry proper opportunity for defence was not given 
to the workman by the E. 0. The management did not 
produce before the F. O., the Investigating Officer|Investigat- 
ing Report of the Security Officer nor did it piodtice the 
Security Officer who held the preliminary investigation as 
u witness in the inquiry. The management also did not 
produce she complainant to confirm his complaint at the 
inquiry.- Lastly, it is alleged by the Union that the finding 
given by the E.O. and accepted by (be Disciplinary Authority, 
and confirmed by the Appellate Authority is not supported 
by evidence which was adduced before (he E.O. These 
officers tailed to take note of the affidavit of the complainant 
dt. 12-3-83 in which it was clearly (dated by the complainant 
that the money was given by him to Sri K. 13. Rawat sub 
staff as u loan for the marriage of the niece of Sri Rawat. 
Union has. therefore, prayed for setting aside the order of 
punishment and for restoration of one increment withheld 
premanently under the order of punishment and all other 
consequential benefits. 

6 . The case it, contested by the management. The manage¬ 
ment plead that vide para 19.14 of the 1st B.P.S. read with: 
Para 3(ii) of the 3rd Bipartite Settlement, the thairman-cum- 
Managing Dilector, who is also (he Chief Executive Office; 
of the Bank is empowered to appoint Disciplinaty Authority 
in respect of the Award Staff. In petsuance of the said 
powers ihe Chairman-cum-Menacing Director ' ide his order 


dt, 7-12-81 copy Eat, M.2 appointed the Regional Managers 
us Disciplinary AuthuiiU. I tie Management dispute the fad 
LbaL the Board of Direciuis of the bank are required to pass 
A resolution empowering Chairman cum-Managing Dircc- 
roi to appoint disciplinary authority and inquiry officer. 
'Hie 3rd Bipartite Settlement clcaily lay* do,vn that the disci¬ 
plinary authority conduct inquiry himself oi appoint another 
officer us fc.O. for the purposes of conducting the inquiry. 
The management bank deny that the workman way not given 
pioper opportunity lor defence. According to the manage¬ 
ment the inquiry against the workman „vus conducted in 
accordance with the procedure laid down in Sastxy Award, 
Dcsai Award ’and the Bipartite Settlement, The management 
also deny the fact that the findings given by Ihe E, O. is 
perverse. According to the management the findings is fully 
supported by evidence on record. 

7. On 2-4-91, the following preliminary issue was framed 
m this case .— 

Whcthct the inquiry was not conducted fairly and pro¬ 
perly in accordance with the principles of natural 
justice ? 

Oil the above issue, the Union examined the workman and 
management examined M. W. Kamta Prasad, Manager 
Mauion Branch and tiled papers relating to inqu'fy 1 may 
state here that the case has been argued not only on the 
preliminary issue but also on the point whether or not the 
finding given by the E.O. is prevetse, 

8 . Ihe tirst point that has been argued by Sti V, K. 
Srivastava, autnorised representative for the Union is that 
the Regional Manager was not appointed as Disciplinary 
Authoniy by Board of Directum. In this argument I find no 
lorce. Unucr the Head Discipline y Action and Procedure 
in ihe 111 Bipartite Settlement it is dpecificully provided that 
the Chief Executive Olhcer ot the Puneipal Officer in India 
of a Bank or an alternate officer at the Flead Office or 
Principal Officer nominated hy him for the purposes shall 
decide which officer tie. the Disciplinaty Authority) shall 
be empoweied to lake disciplinary action in the case of each 
office or establishment. He shall also decide which officer 
or body higher in status than the officer authorised to take 
disciplinary action shall act us the Appellate Authority, It 
further provides that these authorities shall be nominated 
by designation to pass original orders or hear und dispose 
off appeal from time to lime and a notice specifying the 
authorities so nominated shall be published from time to 
time on the Bank's Notice Board. It further Jays down that 
the disciplinary authority may conduct the inquiry himself 
or appoint another officer as inquiry officer for the purpose 
ot conducting an inquiry, 

9. Ext. M-4 is the copy ot notificalioii issued by the 
Chuirman-cuni-Maniiging Director, in pers ounce of the ubove 
provisions contained in the Isl Bipartite Settlement as modi¬ 
fied by 3rd Bipartite Settlement, By means of the said noti¬ 
fication the Regional Manager was appointed as Disciplinary 
Authority in respect of the Award Staff, the document hat, 
been admitted by Sri Srivastava. the 'authorised lepresentgtive 
for the Union, so on this point the Union tails, 

10. the second point argued by Sii Srivastava is that the 
Regional Manager, Lucknow, was not empowered to uppoint 
an officer posted outside his region as inquiry officer. Ad¬ 
mittedly Sri B, Kumar, the second E. O. a, the time of his 
appointment^ as a E.O. was posted as industrial Relation 
Officer. Central Office Bombay, The authorised representa¬ 
tive 1 oi the management say s that iheic it, no bar to such 
appointment by the Disciplinary Authoniy and even other¬ 
wise it is not o^en to the workman,Union to raise such a 
plea as at no time during the inquiry such an objection 
was taken by Ihe wot km an defence representative. The 
workman must therefore h; deemed to have acquired in 
his appointment. 

11 . J have gone thiough the relevant provisions of the 
1st BPS. relating to discipbnuiy proceedings. These settle¬ 
ments lire silent on the point under consideration. Normally, 
in my view, the Regional Manager, should not appoint an 
office! who is posted outside his Region as E.O, If, an in 
the instant case, he appointed an outside officer as E.O., fie

[Part II— Sec. 3fi)] 


18. Hold therefore, that the finding is not perverse. 


committed i;o illegality. Even otherwise it is now not open 
iiv the Union to raise such a plea when no such objection 
wt.'j raised by the \.oikni.in|defenee representative against 
tee oppouiiitment. Having acquired in his .appointment and 
in the conduct of inquuy by him, the Union has lost all 
the grounds to raise such a pica. 

12. Now 1 come to the finding lccoided by the E. O. 
and accepted by the Disciplinary authority and confirmed 
by tile Appellate Authority. Atter hearing the two sides 
and going through the evidence on record and the inquiiy I 
find that there is no sulficient material to hold that the 
finding is perverse or it, not based on evidence or that such 
a view as was taken by the E.O. could not be taken on 
the evidence on record. 

13. At the inquiry the management examined M.W.l, Sri 
A. D. Ktipat, the then manager Madion Branch M.W. 2. 
Smt, Tulsa Devi, M.W.3, Sri Badri Prasad Yadav and M.W.4, 
Sri Sarjoo Prasad Yadav. The last three witnesses arc the 
mother, brother-in-law, and lather respectively of the com¬ 
plainant Kajendra Prasad Yadav. The manager is not a 
witness of tact but the other three witnesses arc witnesses 
oi tact and they have supported the charge by their evidence 
and 1 find nothing in their evidence to disbelieve them. The 
first paymenL of Rs. 500 was witnessed by the mother, 
the second payment of Rs. 500 was witnessed by the father 
and the 3rd payment of Rs, 500 was witnessed by the 
brotlicr-in-i'jw (Bahnoi). 

14. 11 has been argued by Sii Siivastuva, that the manage¬ 
ment did not examine the complainant at the inquiiy. Even 
the EG. did not considered the afiiduvit dt. 12-3-83 of the 
complainant which was filed at the inquiry before the E.O. 
on behalf of the workman. In the affidavit he has said that 
the amount of Rs. 1500 was taken from him by Sri K. D. 
Ruwat as a loan in connection with the marriage of his niece. 

15. 1 do not find any force in this subcniiisiou of Sri Srivas- 
tava. Cogent reasons have been given by the mother, father 
and the brother-in-law of the crnopluinant in their evidence 
about the complaint. Further the affidavit as such is of no 
co./sequence and it could not have been looked into as a 
piece of evidence by the E.O. The receipt of Rs. 1500 was 
admitted by Sri K. D. Rawat sub staff in his reply. If the 
workman or Sri Rawat wanted to rely upon the affidavit of 
the complainant they 'should have produced the complainant 
in the witness box. It appeurs from the evidence and circum¬ 
stances that the workman and Sri K. D. Rawat deliberately 
withheld the complainant. I see no reason why these three 
very closed relatives of the complainant would give a wrong 
statement, it is in their evidence that Rs. 500 were collect¬ 
ed by the complainant fiom his Sasural. Rs, 500 from his 
lather and Rs. 500 from his brother-in-law. Sri K. D, 
Rawat ai; is the case of the workman would not have ap¬ 
proached the complainant for loan in connection with the 
marriage of his niece who himself was in search of employ¬ 
ment and had no money of his own. The fact that the 
workman was also present at the time of receipt of the 
money go lo fihow even if he had not taken the money in his 
hand from the complainant, is a circumstance to show that 
he was a party to the episode. Hence, T find that 
it was rightly held by the E.O. that the workman was also 
a party of the tuking of bribe from the complainant. 

Hi. Arother point which has been raised by Sri Srivaslava, 
is that at the time of inquiry in the list of documents and 
the witnesses the presenting officer included the name of 
Ihe Security Officer who held the preliminary Investigation 
and submitted his report and that the copy of the report 
was demanded hy the defence representative hut he deleted 
both the documents and the name of the security officer from 
the said list saying that he would not rely on them. It was 
on the said liobmission of the Presiding Officer that 
the E.O. observed that since the documents and the witness 
were not being relied upon by the P.O., to substantiate the 
charge, it is not essential to order the P.O. to furnish the 
copy of the investigation report. 

J 7. There is no dispute about Ihe facts, I fail to understand 
how the workman stood prejudiced on account of it when 
the management [bank did not rely on the said document 
and the witness. 


19. Since it is not a case of dismissal, discharge or re¬ 
moval from the service, ihe powers of the Tribunal under 
sec. II-A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, cannot 
invoked. The Appellate Authority has already taken a lenient 
view in the matter and has awarded the workman the punish¬ 
ment of stoppage of one increment, permanently. 

20. Held that the action of the management of the Bank 
of India in imposing the penalty of stoppage of one incre¬ 
ment permanently to Sri Rattan Kumar, wotkman concerned 
is neither illegal nor unjustified. Consequently, the workman 
Union is held entitled to no relief. 

21. Reference is answered accordingly, 

ARJAN DEV, Presiding Officer

BETWEEN 

Kumari Sccma Baral; House No, 24 Garhi Chnvani, 
Dehradun. 

AND 

Tile Senior Divisional Manager, New lndij Assurance
